# LSAT U burgundy booking confirmation

## Summary

Match the supplied Calendly reference using LSAT U branding and its existing burgundy palette. The email body and confirmation page will use burgundy instead of blue. Gmail’s generated event panel may remain blue because Google controls that interface.

## Visual specification

- Use LSAT U’s production booking color:
  - Primary: `hsl(347 70% 27%)`, approximately `#75152A`
  - Dark gradient: `#4F0D1C → #6E1423 → #8E1D3A`
  - White button and ribbon text
- Use exact inline hex colors in email HTML because email clients cannot access the website’s CSS variables.
- Preserve the reference’s white background, generous spacing, narrow content column, understated dividers, and simple typography.
- Replace the blue Calendly logo with the LSAT U mark in burgundy.
- Use burgundy for buttons, links, important icons, and the “Powered by LSAT U” ribbon.
- Do not make the entire email burgundy or reproduce any Calendly branding.

## Confirmation email

- Recreate the reference structure:
  - Centered burgundy LSAT U logo
  - Light divider
  - `Hi [first name],`
  - `Your LSAT U session has been scheduled.`
  - Event type
  - Invitee name and email
  - Date, time, duration, and timezone
  - Google Meet location
- Change the outdated message to: “Your Google Calendar invitation has been sent.”
- Keep a burgundy Google Meet button and plain fallback link.
- Use booking-derived duration and timezone instead of hardcoded values.
- Build with email-safe tables and inline styles for Gmail, Outlook, Apple Mail, and mobile clients.

## Gmail event panel

- Continue sending the actual Google Calendar invitation automatically with the attendee and Meet link.
- Add validated `EventReservation` metadata and a secure reservation action.
- Register the production sender with Google if LSAT U qualifies for enhanced Gmail markup.
- Accept that Gmail controls the generated panel’s blue background, button labels, conflict warnings, and final appearance. LSAT U cannot recolor Gmail’s own interface.

## Confirmation page

- Show “You are scheduled!” immediately after payment.
- Use the same burgundy palette for the success icon, calendar button, links, borders, and diagonal “Powered by LSAT U” ribbon.
- Display attendee, session type, date, time, duration, timezone, and conferencing information.
- Provide one calendar-only “Open Invitation” action.
- Continue polling while the calendar invitation is prepared, then enable the button automatically.
- Show a recoverable confirmed state if Google Calendar is delayed.

## Backend and verification

- Persist the selected timezone.
- Extend the protected booking-status response with the display details, calendar readiness, and calendar URL.
- Allow the outbox worker to send newly unblocked confirmation emails during the same run after calendar creation.
- Preserve payment, calendar, invitation, and email idempotency.
- Compare desktop and mobile email renders against the supplied screenshot.
- Test Gmail, Outlook, Apple Mail, different durations and timezones, delayed calendar creation, and invalid booking tokens.
- Perform one separately authorized $1 production booking after deployment.
- Obtain fresh confirmation before any Lovable action that could consume credits.

## Assumptions

- “Instead of blue” applies to all LSAT U-controlled email and confirmation-page elements.
- Gmail’s surrounding interface and generated event panel are excluded because their colors cannot be controlled by LSAT U.

## developer

You are an expert code reviewer specializing in modern software development across multiple languages and frameworks. Your primary responsibility is to review code against project guidelines in AGENTS.md with high precision to minimize false positives.

## Review Scope

By default, review unstaged changes from `git diff`. The user may specify different files or scope to review.

## Core Review Responsibilities

**Project Guidelines Compliance**: Verify adherence to explicit project rules (typically in AGENTS.md or equivalent) including import patterns, framework conventions, language-specific style, function declarations, error handling, logging, testing practices, platform compatibility, and naming conventions.

**Bug Detection**: Identify actual bugs that will impact functionality - logic errors, null/undefined handling, race conditions, memory leaks, security vulnerabilities, and performance problems.

**Code Quality**: Evaluate significant issues like code duplication, missing critical error handling, accessibility problems, and inadequate test coverage.

## Issue Confidence Scoring

Rate each issue from 0-100:

- **0-25**: Likely false positive or pre-existing issue
- **26-50**: Minor nitpick not explicitly in AGENTS.md
- **51-75**: Valid but low-impact issue
- **76-90**: Important issue requiring attention
- **91-100**: Critical bug or explicit AGENTS.md violation

**Only report issues with confidence ≥ 80**

## Output Format

Start by listing what you're reviewing. For each high-confidence issue provide:

- Clear description and confidence score
- File path and line number
- Specific AGENTS.md rule or bug explanation
- Concrete fix suggestion

Group issues by severity (Critical: 90-100, Important: 80-89).

If no high-confidence issues exist, confirm the code meets standards with a brief summary.

Be thorough but filter aggressively - quality over quantity. Focus on issues that truly matter.

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

## developer

You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.

## developer

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 10.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "1:-- Persist the facts shown after checkout and remove the legacy $45-only\r\n2:-- assumption from paid booking finalization.\r\n3:\r\n4:ALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings\r\n5:  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS booker_timezone text,\r\n6:  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS quoted_amount_cents integer,\r\n7:  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS quoted_currency text;\r\n8:\r\n9:UPDATE public.single_session_bookings\r\n10:SET booker_timezone = COALESCE(booker_timezone, 'America/Toronto')\r\n11:WHERE booker_timezone IS NULL;\r\n12:\r\n13:ALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings\r\n14:  ALTER COLUMN booker_timezone SET DEFAULT 'America/Toronto',\r\n15:  ALTER COLUMN booker_timezone SET NOT NULL;\r\n16:\r\n17:ALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings\r\n18:  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS single_session_quoted_amount_nonnegative_chk,\r\n19:  ADD CONSTRAINT single_session_quoted_amount_nonnegative_chk\r\n20:    CHECK (quoted_amount_cents IS NULL OR quoted_amount_cents >= 0);\r\n21:\r\n22:-- Keep the proven slot-claim transaction intact behind a private compatibility\r\n23:-- function, then atomically attach the validated display timezone and immutable\r\n24:-- checkout quote to the returned booking.\r\n25:ALTER FUNCTION public.claim_single_session_slot(\r\n26:  timestamptz, text, text, text, jsonb, text, text, text, uuid, text\r\n27:) RENAME TO claim_single_session_slot_without_confirmation_details;\r\n28:\r\n29:REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.claim_single_session_slot_without_confirmation_details(\r\n30:  timestamptz, text, text, text, jsonb, text, text, text, uuid, text\r\n31:) FROM public, anon, authenticated, service_role;\r\n32:\r\n33:CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.claim_single_session_slot(\r\n34:  _start_at timestamptz,\r\n35:  _name text,\r\n36:  _email text,\r\n37:  _notes text,\r\n38:  _guests jsonb,\r\n39:  _client_idempotency_key text,\r\n40:  _booking_token_hash text,\r\n41:  _ip_hash text,\r\n42:  _user_id uuid DEFAULT NULL,\r\n43:  _event_slug text DEFAULT 'single-session',\r\n44:  _booker_timezone text DEFAULT 'America/Toronto'\r\n45:) RETURNS public.single_session_bookings\r\n46:LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER\r\n47:SET search_path = public\r\n48:AS $$\r\n49:DECLARE\r\n50:  _booking public.single_session_bookings%ROWTYPE;\r\n51:  _timezone text := NULLIF(trim(_booker_timezone), '');\r\n52:  _amount integer;\r\n53:  _currency text;\r\n54:BEGIN\r\n55:  IF _timezone IS NULL OR NOT EXISTS (\r\n56:    SELECT 1 FROM pg_catalog.pg_timezone_names WHERE name = _timezone\r\n57:  ) THEN\r\n58:    RAISE EXCEPTION 'invalid_timezone' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\r\n59:  END IF;\r\n60:\r\n61:  _booking := public.claim_single_session_slot_without_confirmation_details(\r\n62:    _start_at, _name, _email, _notes, _guests, _client_idempotency_key,\r\n63:    _booking_token_hash, _ip_hash, _user_id, _event_slug\r\n64:  );\r\n65:\r\n66:  _amount := public.booking_price_cents(_booking.event_slug, _booking.email);\r\n67:  SELECT lower(currency) INTO _currency\r\n68:  FROM public.booking_event_types WHERE slug = _booking.event_slug;\r\n69:  IF _amount IS NULL OR _currency IS NULL THEN\r\n70:    RAISE EXCEPTION 'invalid_event_price' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\r\n71:  END IF;\r\n72:\r\n73:  UPDATE public.single_session_bookings b\r\n74:  SET booker_timezone = COALESCE(b.booker_timezone, _timezone),\r\n75:      quoted_amount_cents = COALESCE(b.quoted_amount_cents, _amount),\r\n76:      quoted_currency = COALESCE(b.quoted_currency, _currency)\r\n77:  WHERE b.id = _booking.id\r\n78:  RETURNING * INTO _booking;\r\n79:\r\n80:  RETURN _booking;\r\n81:END;\r\n82:$$;\r\n83:\r\n84:REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.claim_single_session_slot(\r\n85:  timestamptz, text, text, text, jsonb, text, text, text, uuid, text, text\r\n86:) FROM public, anon, authenticated;\r\n87:GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.claim_single_session_slot(\r\n88:  timestamptz, text, text, text, jsonb, text, text, jsonb_strip_nulls(jsonb_build_object(\r\n138:    'id', _booking.id,\r\n139:    'booking_id', _booking.id,\r\n140:    'status', _booking.status,\r\n141:    'start_at', _booking.start_at,\r\n142:    'end_at', _booking.end_at,\r\n143:    'attendee_name', _booking.name,\r\n144:    'event_label', COALESCE(_event.label, 'LSAT Session'),\r\n145:    'duration_minutes', round(extract(epoch FROM (_booking.end_at - _booking.start_at)) / 60)::integer,\r\n146:    'timezone', COALESCE(_booking.booker_timezone, 'America/Toronto'),\r\n147:    'meet_url', CASE WHEN _booking.status = 'booked' THEN _booking.meet_url ELSE NULL END,\r\n148:    'calendar_url', CASE WHEN _booking.status = 'booked' THEN _calendar_url ELSE NULL END,\r\n149:    'calendar_ready', _calendar_ready,\r\n150:    'calendar_pending', (_booking.status = 'booked' AND NOT _calendar_ready)\r\n151:  ));\r\n152:END;\r\n153:$$;\r\n154:\r\n155:REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.get_single_session_status(text, text, public.finalize_single_session_payment(\r\n163:  _booking_id uuid,\r\n164:  _provider_event_id text,\r\n165:  _stripe_checkout_session_id text,\r\n166:  _stripe_payment_intent_id text,\r\n167: integer;\r\n185:  _expected_currency text;\r\n186:BEGIN\r\n187:  IF NULLIF(_provider_event_id, '') IS NULL\r\n188:     OR NULLIF(_stripe_checkout_session_id, '') IS NULL\r\n189:     OR NULLIF(_stripe_payment_intent_id, '') IS NULL\r\n190:     OR _amount_cents IS NULL OR _amount_cents < 0\r\n191:     OR NULLIF(lower(COALESCE(_currency, '')), '') IS NULL\r\n192:     OR _livemode IS _event FROM public.booking_event_types WHERE slug = _booking.event_slug;\r\n201:  IF NOT FOUND OR _event.requires_payment IS DISTINCT FROM true THEN\r\n202:    RAISE EXCEPTION 'payment_not_required' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\r\n203:  END IF;\r\n204:\r\n205:  _expected_amount := COALESCE(\r\n206:    _booking.quoted_amount_cents,\r\n207:    public.booking_price_cents(_booking.event_slug, _stripe_payment_intent_id\r\n229:       OR _booking.amount_cents IS DISTINCT FROM _amount_cents\r\n230:       OR lower(_booking.currency) IS DISTINCT FROM lower(_currency)\r\n231:       OR provider_event_id, event_type, offer_id, amount_cents, currency,\r\n253:    payload, single_session_booking_id\r\n254:  ) VALUES (\r\n255:    'stripe', _provider_event_id, 'single_session_paid', _booking.event_slug,\r\n256:    _amount_cents, lower(_currency),\r\n257:    jsonb_build_object('livemode', _livemode, 'checkout_session_id', _stripe_checkout_session_id),\r\n258:    _booking_id\r\n259:  ) ON CONFLICT (provider, provider_event_id, event_type) DO NOTHING;\r\n260:\r\n261:  _can_book := _live_calendar_clear\r\n262:    AND (_booking.status = 'checkout_open'\r\n263:      OR (_booking.status = 'expired' AND _booking.status_changed_at >= now() - interval '10 minutes'))\r\n264:    AND public.booking_slot_is_clear(\r\n265:      _booking.start_at, _booking.end_at,\r\n266:      COALESCE(_event.buffer_before_minutes, 0), COALESCE(_event.buffer_after_minutes, 0),\r\n267:      _booking.id, _booking.gcal_event_id, NULL\r\n268:    );\r\n269:\r\n270:  IF _can_book THEN\r\n271:    IF NULLIF(_cancel_token_hash, '') IS NULL OR NULLIF(_cancel_payload_ciphertext, '') IS NULL THEN\r\n272:      RAISE EXCEPTION 'cancel_capability_required' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\r\n273:    END IF;\r\n274:    UPDATE public.single_session_bookings b SET\r\n275:      status = 'booked', lifecycle_version = b.lifecycle_version + 1,\r\n276:      stripe_payment_intent_id = _stripe_payment_intent_id,\r\n277:      amount_cents = _amount_cents, currency = lower(_currency), livemode = _livemode,\r\n278:      cancel_token_hash = _cancel_token_hash, cancel_token_expires_at = b.start_at,\r\n279:      terminal_reason = NULL\r\n280:    WHERE b.id = _booking_id RETURNING * INTO _booking;\r\n281:    _new_version := _booking.lifecycle_version;\r\n282:\r\n283:    PERFORM public.enqueue_single_session_outbox(_booking.id, 'gcal_promote', 'system', 'pending',\r\n284:      'booked', _new_version, NULL);\r\n285:    PERFORM public.enqueue_single_session_outbox(_booking.id, 'email_admin_notification', 'admin', 'blocked',\r\n288:      'booked', _new_version, NULL);\r\n289:  ELSE\r\n290:    UPDATE public.single_session_bookings b SET\r\n291:      status = 'refund_pending', _livemode,\r\n294:      terminal_reason = 'paid_slot_unavailable'\r\n295:    WHERE b.id = _booking_id RETURNING * INTO _booking;\r\n296:    _new_version := _booking.lifecycle_version;\r\n297:    UPDATE public.single_session_outbox SET state = 'superseded', updated_at = now()\r\n298:      WHERE booking_id = _booking.id AND state IN ('blocked', 'pending', 'failed')\r\n299:        AND action IN ('gcal_promote', 'email_booking_confirmation', 'email_admin_notification');\r\n300:    PERFORM public.enqueue_single_session_outbox(_booking.id, 'gcal_delete', 'system', 'pending',\r\n301: booking_passes_reserved_session_slug_chk;\r\n106:\r\n107:CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.claim_single_session_outbox(\r\n108:  _worker_id text,\r\n109:  _actions text[] DEFAULT NULL,\r\n110:  _limit integer DEFAULT 20,\r\n111:  _lease_seconds integer DEFAULT 120\r\n112:) RETURNS SETOF public.single_session_outbox\r\n113:LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public AS $$\r\n114:BEGIN\r\n115:  IF NULLIF(trim(_worker_id), '') IS NULL OR _limit NOT BETWEEN 1 AND 100\r\n116:     OR _lease_seconds NOT BETWEEN 10 AND 900 THEN\r\n117:    RAISE EXCEPTION 'invalid_lease_request' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\r\n118:  END IF;\r\n119:\r\n120:  UPDATE public.single_session_outbox o SET\r\n121:    state = 'superseded', lease_worker_id = NULL, lease_token = NULL,\r\n122:    lease_until = NULL, updated_at = now(),\r\n123:    last_error = COALESCE(o.last_error, 'lifecycle precondition no longer holds')\r\n124:  FROM public.single_session_bookings b\r\n125:  WHERE b.id = o.booking_id\r\n126:    AND o.state IN ('pending', 'failed', 'leased')\r\n127:    AND (o.state <> 'leased' OR o.lease_until <= now())\r\n128:    AND (b.status <> o.precondition_status\r\n129:      OR b.lifecycle_version <> o.precondition_lifecycle_version);\r\n130:\r\n131:  RETURN QUERY\r\n132:  WITH candidates AS (\r\n133:    SELECT o.id\r\n134:    FROM public.single_session_outbox o\r\n135:    WHERE (o.state = 'pending'\r\n136:        OR (o.state = 'leased' AND o.lease_until <= now()))\r\n137:      AND o.next_retry_at <= now()\r\n138:      AND o.attempts < o.max_attempts\r\n139:      AND (_actions IS NULL OR o.action = ANY(_actions))\r\n140:    ORDER BY o.next_retry_at, o.created_at\r\n141:    FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED\r\n142:    LIMIT _limit\r\n143:  )\r\n144:  UPDATE public.single_session_outbox o SET\r\n145:    state = 'leased', attempts = o.attempts + 1,\r\n146:    lease_worker_id = _worker_id, lease_token = gen_random_uuid(),\r\n147:    lease_until = now() + make_interval(secs => _lease_seconds), updated_at = now()\r\n148:  FROM candidates c WHERE o.id = c.id\r\n149:  RETURNING o.*;\r\n150:END;\r\n151:$$;\r\n152:\r\n153:CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.complete_single_session_outbox(\r\n154:  _job_id uuid,\r\n155:  _lease_token uuid,\r\n156:  _provider_status text DEFAULT NULL,\r\n157:  _result jsonb DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,\r\n158:  _meet_url text DEFAULT NULL\r\n159:) RETURNS jsonb\r\n160:LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public AS $$\r\n161:DECLARE\r\n162:  _job public.single_session_outbox%ROWTYPE;\r\n163:  _booking public.single_session_bookings%ROWTYPE;\r\n164:  _compensate boolean := false;\r\n165:  _refund_id text;\r\n166:BEGIN\r\n167:  SELECT * INTO _job FROM public.single_session_outbox WHERE id = _job_id FOR UPDATE;\r\n168:  IF NOT FOUND THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'outbox_job_not_found' USING ERRCODE = 'P0404'; END IF;\r\n169:  IF _job.state <> 'leased' OR _job.lease_token IS DISTINCT FROM _lease_token\r\n170:     OR _job.lease_until <= now() THEN\r\n171:    RAISE EXCEPTION 'outbox_lease_lost' USING ERRCODE = 'P0409';\r\n172:  END IF;\r\n173:\r\n174:  PERFORM pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('single-session-side-effect:' || _job.booking_id::text, 0));\r\n175:  SELECT * INTO _booking FROM public.single_session_bookings WHERE id = _job.booking_id FOR UPDATE;\r\n176:  IF _booking.status <> _job.precondition_status\r\n177:     OR _booking.lifecycle_version <> _job.precondition_lifecycle_version THEN\r\n178:    _compensate := _job.action = 'gcal_promote';\r\n179:    UPDATE public.single_session_outbox SET\r\n180:      state = 'superseded', lease_worker_id = NULL, lease_token = NULL,\r\n181:      lease_until = NULL, last_error = 'lifecycle changed during side effect', updated_at = now()\r\n182:    WHERE id = _job_id;\r\n183:    RETURN jsonb_build_object('applied', false, 'compensate', _compensate);\r\n184:  END IF;\r\n185:\r\n186:  IF _job.action = 'gcal_promote' THEN\r\n187:    IF NULLIF(_meet_url, '') IS NULL THEN\r\n188:      RAISE EXCEPTION 'meet_url_required' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\r\n189:    END IF;\r\n190:    UPDATE public.single_session_bookings SET meet_url = _meet_url WHERE id = _booking.id;\r\n191:    UPDATE public.single_session_outbox SET state = 'pending', next_retry_at = now(), updated_at = now()\r\n192:    WHERE booking_id = _booking.id AND state = 'blocked'\r\n193:      AND action IN ('email_booking_confirmation', 'email_admin_notification')\r\n194:      AND precondition_status = _booking.status\r\n195:      AND precondition_lifecycle_version = _booking.lifecycle_version;\r\n196:  ELSIF _job.action = 'stripe_refund' THEN\r\n197:    _refund_id := NULLIF(_result->>'refund_id', '');\r\n198:    IF _refund_id IS NOT NULL THEN\r\n199:      UPDATE public.single_session_bookings SET\r\n200:        stripe_refund_id = COALESCE(stripe_refund_id, _refund_id),\r\n201:        stripe_refund_status = COALESCE(_provider_status, stripe_refund_status)\r\n202:      WHERE id = _booking.id;\r\n203:    END IF;\r\n204:    IF lower(COALESCE(_provider_status, '')) = 'succeeded' THEN\r\n205:      UPDATE public.single_session_bookings b SET\r\n206:        status = 'refunded', lifecycle_version = b.lifecycle_version + 1,\r\n207:        stripe_refund_status = 'succeeded'\r\n208:      WHERE b.id = _booking.id RETURNING * INTO _booking;\r\n209:      PERFORM public.enqueue_single_session_outbox(_booking.id, 'email_refund_apology', 'booker', 'pending',\r\n210:        'refunded', _booking.lifecycle_version, NULL);\r\n211:    END IF;\r\n212:  END IF;\r\n213:\r\n214:  UPDATE public.single_session_outbox SET\r\n215:    state = 'succeeded', provider_status = _provider_status,\r\n216:    result = COALESCE(_result, '{}'::jsonb), payload_ciphertext = NULL,\r\n217:    sensitive_payload_purged_at = CASE WHEN payload_ciphertext IS NULL\r\n218:      THEN sensitive_payload_purged_at ELSE now() END,\r\n219:    lease_worker_id = NULL, lease_token = The following declaration adds a `ViewAction` button to an email: ...         "url": "https://play.google.com", ... You can validate your markup using the Email Markup Tester Tool.\n\n# Go-to actions Stay organized with collections Save and categorize content based on your preferences.\n\nGo-to actions take the user to your website where the action can be completed. Unlike one-click actions, go-to actions can be interacted with multiple times.\n\nImage: Go-to actions in Gmail\n\n## Use cases\n\nGo-to actions supported by Gmail are:\n\n  * ViewAction\n  * TrackAction\n\nMore actions might be supported in the future.\n\nNote: Before you start, review how to embed schemas in emails and test schemas.\n\n## View Action\n\nYou can add a `ViewAction` button to emails requiring users to go to your site to complete the action.\n\nThe following declaration adds a `ViewAction` button to an email:\n\n### JSON-LD\n    \n    `<scrip t t ype="application/ld+json">\n    {\n      "@context": "http://schema.org",\n      "@type": "EmailMessage",\n      "potentialAction": {\n        "@type": "ViewAction",\n        "url": "https://watch-movies.com/watch?movieId=abc123",\n        "name": "Watch movie"\n      },\n      "description": "Watch the \'Avengers\' movie online"\n    }\n    </scrip t>\n    \n    `\n\n### Microdata\n    \n    `<div itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/EmailMessage">\n      <div itemprop="potentialAction" itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/ViewAction">\n        <link itemprop="target" href="https://watch-movies.com/watch?movieId=abc123"/>\n        <meta itemprop="name" content="Watch movie"/>\n      </div>\n      <meta itemprop="description" content="Watch the \'Avengers\' movie online"/>\n    </div>\n    \n    `\n\n### Publisher data\n\nYou can add details about the organization sending the email message by setting the `publisher` field:\n\n### JSON-LD\n    \n    `<scrip t t ype="application/ld+json">\n    {\n      "@context": "http://schema.org",\n      "@type": "EmailMessage",\n      "description": "Watch the \'Avengers\' movie online",\n      "potentialAction": {\n        "@type": "ViewAction",\n        "url": "https://watch-movies.com/watch?movieId=abc123",\n        "name": "Watch movie"\n      },\n      "publisher": {\n        "@type": "Organization",\n        "name": "Google Play",\n        "url": "https://play.google.com",\n        "url/googlePlus": "https://plus.google.com/106886664866983861036"\n      }\n    }\n    </scrip t>\n    \n    `\n\n### Microdata\n    \n    `<div itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/EmailMessage">\n      <meta itemprop="description" content="Watch the \'Avengers\' movie online"/>\n      <div itemprop="potentialAction" itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/ViewAction">\n        <link itemprop="target" href="https://watch-movies.com/watch?movieId=abc123"/>\n        <meta itemprop="name" content="Watch movie"/>\n      </div>\n      <div itemprop="publisher" itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/Organization">\n        <meta itemprop="name" content="Google Play"/>\n        <link itemprop="url" href="https://play.google.com"/>\n        <link itemprop="url/googlePlus" href="https://plus.google.com/106886664866983861036"/>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n    \n    `\n\n## Track Action\n\nYou can add a `TrackAction` button to emails requiring users to go to your site to track packages being delivered.\n\nAn action is automatically generated when you specify the `trackingUrl` property. To link directly to a mobile application, also include a `TrackAction` as shown in the following example:\n\n### JSON-LD\n    \n    `<scrip t t ype="application/ld+json">\n    {\n      "@context": "http://schema.org",\n      "@type": "ParcelDelivery",\n      "deliveryAddress": {\n        "@type": "PostalAddress",\n        "streetAddress": "24 Willie Mays Plaza",\n        "addressLocality": "San Francisco",\n        "addressRegion": "CA",\n        "addressCountry": "US",\n        "postalCode": "94107"\n      },\n      "expectedArrivalUntil": "2013-03-12T12:00:00-08:00",\n      "carrier": {\n        "@type": "Organization",\n        "name": "FedEx"\n      },\n      "itemShipped": {\n        "@type": "Product",\n        "name": "iPod Mini"\n      },\n      "partOfOrder": {\n        "@type": "Order",\n        "orderNumber": "176057",\n        "merchant": {\n          "@type": "Organization",\n          "name": "Bob Dole"\n        }\n      },\n      "trackingUrl": "http://fedex.com/track/1234567890"\n        <meta itemprop="orderNumber" content="176057"/>\n        <div itemprop="merchant" itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/Organization">\n          <meta itemprop="name" content="Bob Dole"/>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n      <link itemprop="trackingUrl" href="http://fedex.com/track/1234567890"/>\n      <div itemprop="potentialAction" itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/TrackAction">\n        <link itemprop="target" href="http://fedex.com/track/1234567890"/>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n    \n    `\n\n## Test your markup\n\nYou can validate your markup using the Email Markup Tester Tool. Paste in your markup code and click the Validate button to scan the content and receive a report on any errors present.\n\n## Specification\n\nFor a specification of the various go-to action types, see the documentation for the specific type ViewAction or TrackAction.\n\nNote: Some schemas that Google uses are still going through the standardization process of Schema.org and might change in the future. You declare in-app actions like go-to actions, but include extra information that helps email clients (such as Gmail) handle the action inline. ... When the user clicks the button, Google sends an HTTP request to your service, recording the confirmation.\n\n# Declare actions Stay organized with collections Save and categorize content based on your preferences.\n\nIn schema.org, an action represents an activity that can be performed on a piece of structured data. Gmail supports multiple types of actions, and you can define each with similar structured data.\n\n## Go-to actions\n\nIf you add markup to your content with schema.org entities, you can add go-to actions for them. For example, to give an `EmailMessage` entity a `ViewAction` go-to link, populate the email\'s `potentialAction` property, as in the following example:\n\n### JSON-LD\n    \n    `<scrip t t ype="application/ld+json">\n    {\n      "@context": "http://schema.org",\n      "@type": "EmailMessage",\n      "potentialAction": {\n        "@type": "ViewAction",\n        "target": "https://watch-movies.com/watch?movieId=abc123",\n        "name": "Watch movie"\n      },\n      "description": "Watch the \'Avengers\' movie online"\n    }\n    </scrip t>\n    \n    `\n\n### Microdata\n    \n    `<div itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/EmailMessage">\n      <div itemprop="potentialAction" itemscope itemtype="http://schema.org/ViewAction">\n        <link itemprop="target" href="https://watch-movies.com/watch?movieId=abc123"/>\n        <meta itemprop="name" content="Watch movie"/>\n      </div>\n      <meta itemprop="description" content="Watch the \'Avengers\' movie online"/>\n    </div>\n    \n    `\n\nOther email clients that don\'t support schemas in emails automatically ignore this markup.\n\n## Mobile deep linking\n\nGo-to actions can also link directly to content in mobile apps on Android and iOS. To deep link to an app, include additional `target` URLs encoded with the `android-app://` and `ios-app://` schemes, as shown in the following examples:\n\n### JSON-LD\n    \n    `"target": [\n      "<web url>",\n      "android-app://<android package name>/<scheme>/<host>/<path+query>",\n      "ios-app://<App store ID>/<scheme>/<host><path+query>"\n    ]\n    `\n\n### Microdata\n    \n    `<link itemprop="target" href="<web url>"/>\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nWhat are actions? \xa0|\xa0 Gmail \xa0|\xa0 Google for Developers (https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/markup/actions/actions-overview)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn12search5\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 4 days ago; For example, when a user registers for a website, they receive an email asking them to confirm their registration. ... Image: A Go-To Action in Gmail ... Note: Some schemas that Google uses are still going through the standardization process of Schema.org and might change in the future.\n\n# What are actions? Stay organized with collections Save and categorize content based on your preferences.\n\nActions enable users to interact with your product or service right inside Gmail.\n\nFor example, when you send a notification that a magazine subscription is about to expire, you can present the option to renew it right from the notification.\n\nYou can declare actions in two ways: in-app actions or go-to actions. Gmail supports multiple types of actions.\n\n## In-app actions\n\nGmail handles in-app actions in place without sending the user to another website. These actions include one-click actions.\n\n### One-click action\n\nIn many scenarios, the user must confirm a predefined request. For example, when a user registers for a website, they receive an email asking them to confirm their registration. Similarly, a movie or music recommendation service can prompt a user to add items to a queue to enjoy later.\n\nImage: A Confirmation Button in Gmail\nOne-click actions in Gmail\n\nTo learn more, see the one-click action reference.\n\n## Go-to actions\n\nFor more complex interactions, use go-to actions to provide a direct link to the page where the user can perform the action. Clicking the button redirects the user to the page specified in the action definition.\n\nUse go-to actions for interactions that require multiple steps or significant data entry that users can\'t perform from the inbox. For example, an airline sending a check-in reminder can add a go-to action to direct the user to the airline website to select a seat or request an upgrade.\n\nImage: A Go-To Action in Gmail\nGo-to actions in Gmail\n\nTo learn more, see the go-to action reference.\n\n## Further reading\n\n  * Declare actions\nNote: Some schemas that Google uses are still going through the standardization process of Schema.org and might change in the future. ## assistant

Reviewed the uncommitted booking confirmation changes across the frontend, Edge Functions, email template, tests, and database migration.

Critical

1. Selected timezone is never persisted correctly. Confidence: 99  
   [20260915170000_booking_confirmation_details.sql](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-wt-booking-confirmation/supabase/migrations/20260915170000_booking_confirmation_details.sql:74)

   The column is backfilled, made `NOT NULL`, and given an `America/Toronto` default before the legacy claim function inserts rows. Therefore `COALESCE(b.booker_timezone, _timezone)` always keeps the default and discards the booker’s selected timezone.

   Fix: initialize timezone based on a first-claim marker such as `quoted_amount_cents IS NULL`, or otherwise distinguish a new claim from an idempotent replay before preserving the existing timezone.

2. The $1 or email-specific price override still fails in the Stripe webhook. Confidence: 98  
   [booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-wt-booking-confirmation/supabase/functions/booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts:248)

   The webhook validates `session.amount_total` against `booking_event_types.price_cents`, ignoring the newly persisted `quoted_amount_cents` and `quoted_currency`. A legitimately quoted $1 checkout for a normally $45 event returns `amount_mismatch` before `finalize_single_session_payment` can use the corrected validation.

   Fix: select the immutable quote from the booking and validate against it, with the event price only as a fallback for older in-flight rows.

Important

3. The Gmail `EventReservation` metadata does not satisfy Gmail’s schema. Confidence: 99  
   [booking-confirmation.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-wt-booking-confirmation/supabase/functions/_shared/transactional-email-templates/booking-confirmation.tsx:67)

   Gmail requires `reservationFor.location` to be a `Place` containing a complete `PostalAddress`. The implementation supplies a `VirtualLocation`, so Google’s email markup validator will reject the reservation markup.

   Because this is genuinely a virtual session, do not invent a physical address. Use an `EmailMessage` with a `potentialAction` `ViewAction`, or omit `EventReservation` and rely on the actual Google Calendar invitation.

4. The confirmation page can stop polling and enable the invitation too early. Confidence: 94  
   [SingleSession.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-wt-booking-confirmation/src/pages/SingleSession.tsx:425) and [SingleSession.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-wt-booking-confirmation/src/pages/SingleSession.tsx:296)

   The calendar mirror row is written before the outbox completion stores the Meet URL. During that interval, or after a completion failure, `calendar_url` can exist while `calendar_ready` remains false. The frontend then stops polling and renders an active “Open Invitation” link despite still describing the invitation as being prepared.

   Fix: base both polling and button availability on `isCalendarReady(state)`, and require readiness plus a URL before enabling the link.
